What does that mean? What are we looking at today? Today we are looking at a draft law which prolongs the operation of certain positions, certain texts and regulations in the Budget Act for 2021. We are not looking at a budget today! Today we are looking at a temporary law that will operate until 31 March 2022. At the same time, we are anxiously expecting today the Minister of Finance to come out and announce the main parameters of the Budget Act for 2022. We look forward to the bright future that will be presented to us in June with the update of the budget of the one who has not yet accepted it of the unborn Petko. At the same time, we do not know, given the situation in which we are in the National Assembly, whether there will be another or two updates by the end of the year. This is what I am saying to ask you to think about everything that is happening in the National Assembly in relation to the approval procedure, respectively the implementation of the state budget for 2022. However, as Mr. Chobanov said, at the same time, Bulgarian citizens expect the things that were regulated by the budget for 2021 by the GERB government to continue until the normal budget is adopted and in which these texts will be re-regulated, and some of them can even be felt at least according to the application of the ruling majority. Unfortunately, we have come to this point because it is the result of a number of crises that have been created in our country and, above all, the political crisis, which did not allow the majority in Parliament to form during two National Assemblys on a regular basis, and therefore we did not have the opportunity to adopt within the year the state budget for 2022. This is a fact, there is no turning back and we should, of course, focus on the draft law which is currently presented to us and which decides a small part of what Bulgarian citizens expect as social payments and economic support of the Bulgarian business. I would like to thank the Committee on Budget and Finance for yesterday's decision, not only on the title, as I refer to the title, but it also applies to many other texts included in the Bill, in which we agreed to drop all the words 'extending to 31 March 2022', because this essentially means continuing certain measures and actions of texts that have completed their action as at 31 December 2021. From this point of view, changing the text with implementation makes it at least a little more constitutional. Otherwise, there was a high probability and our parliamentary group was already preparing the unconstitutional bill. That way, I think we're reaching some kind of consensus. We are addressing Bulgarian citizens to tell them that regardless of the fact that this procedure is really unique, there is no such procedure so far, I mean in this type of Law, and not the introduction later on of the Budget Bill, as there are two years in which such budget has been introduced. Of course, these two years are quite different and relate to another type of political, financial and economic crises. But I suggest that we continue to look at the texts that will continue to act during the first quarter of this year, as I am convinced and when I have written these texts together with my colleagues at the Ministry of Finance that these rights should be granted to the Bulgarian citizens, to the Bulgarian business. ParlaMint-BG_2022-01-21.u450	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Honourable Members of the National Assembly! Since no extraordinary Council of Presidents was called, as we have asked, we have seen ourselves forced to write a declaration in which to express our clear attitude towards what is happening at the moment, which directly affects Bulgarian national security. About a month ago, the Russian Federation published a package of unacceptable ultimative demands for NATO. The most important of them, which directly affect the Republic of Bulgaria, are the demands that NATO withdraw all its equipment, personnel and weapons to the state since 1997. And the second requirement was for NATO to stop its further expansion, practically denying the right to choose sovereign states, such as Ukraine and Georgia, to choose on their own what alliances to join. Such conditions conflict with any established norms in modern international relations. Russia's ambition is obviously to weaken NATO and de facto eliminate the fictitious membership of the Alliance of the whole of Eastern Europe. This Europe, which, after the fall of the Berlin Wall, literally fled to the West so as not to fall back into the hands of an empire, which at that time, and now, offers nothing but outdated understanding of international relations. It is obvious that Soviet imperial reflexes die hard, and lessons from the Caribbean crisis from the 1960s have not been learned. In today's world, there is no way to distribute areas of influence like in the 19th century and trample the will of neighboring nations. The Bulgarians are a sovereign nation and we make our own decisions about the guarantees our country needs. The unacceptable Russian narrative says that someone is playing around with hypotheses that direct the attention of the North Atlantic Alliance directly to art. 5 of the Washington Treaty. Bulgaria is part of a political space whose internal decisions Moscow has long had neither control nor influence. Such provocations make it even more necessary for the Russian Federation to reconsider the signals of aggression that it emits to the surrounding world and to accept the calls of all countries of the Euro-Atlantic space for deescalation and for abstention from the policy of blabbering arms. While NATO categorically rejected the Russian ultimatums, the Bulgarian government was deafeningly silent. Today at a press conference of the Russian Ministry of Foreign Affairs Bulgaria was explicitly mentioned as a country from which NATO should practically withdraw. The Bulgarian institutions are silent again, and the majority in parliament does not understand the importance of what is happening and what problem it represents for the Bulgarian national security. Meanwhile, the defence minister is going to an anti-NATO protest. That is why we insist: Firstly, the government and the president should immediately take a clear and unambiguous position on Russian threats and behaviour. Secondly, Rumen Radev should immediately convene an extraordinary Advisory Council on National Security on the above topics. Thirdly, to convene an urgent meeting of the Defence Committee at which the Defence Minister will inform the MPs of the positions held so far in NATO on the threat of Russian military aggression in Ukraine and to present the government's actions and plans to secure the Bulgarian national defence. Fourth, the government should immediately include in the Budget Act 2% of gross domestic product for our national defence capabilities, without including a bridge over the Danube. Fifth, in a moment of direct threats, the government will seek and win clear messages from our allies that in a moment of direct threats and threats to our country NATO is behind its values and commitments to Bulgaria. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-01-21.u944	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Honourable Members of the National Assembly! I present to you a bill for amending and supplementing the Energy Act. The project of the Law amending and supplementing the Energy Act provides for a reduction in the number of members of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission (s) from nine to five. It is envisaged to reduce the number of members in the Board of Staff from four to two, as well as the elimination of the requirement for one Commissioner with legal experience and one member with experience in the field of economy. In this way a common plenary of five members, of which one Chair, two members with experience in energy and two with experience in water supply and sewerage services, shall be formed. The proposed change optimises the Commission's numerical composition as an independent college body, with significant financial savings to be made, and these funds can be redirected to better expert provision of the Commission. The project envisions wider definitions of legal terms and conditions in the field of energy and water supply and sewerage, recognising as such the internship in European Union bodies as well as traders with similar subject-matter of activity but registered and/or licensed in the territory of another Member State. The changing energy sector requires cross-treatment with information regarding electricity, gas and development of wholesale and retail markets, knowledge and experience, as well as an overview of different market segments and sectors. In this context, the proposed extension of the definition of internship in energy also covers internships in the field of finance, accounting, trade in financial instruments or banking related to the energy sector. Thank you.

The removal of the requirement for legal education of at least one member and one with experience in the field of the economy is contrary to the main activity of the regulator, namely to interpret the legislative texts and to resolve legal and economic disputes. At the same time, the selection of members of the KEWRC, who are owners or managers of energy companies or other licensed companies, which opens the way for conflict of interest, is practically acceptable. We have already seen these in several other ministries and government agencies, as we have pointed out in a number of reports by the Center for the Study of Democracy. What needs to be changed is: - a ban on interfering with the decisions of the KEWRC and the KPC by adopting extraordinary parliamentary and government decisions involving non-existent obligations or bans of the two regulators; - removing the political influence on the selection of the composition and professional team of KEWRC and KPC, eliminating informal party quotas; - the application of the highest criteria for experience in the field of energy, economy and law; - prevent conflict of interest by not allowing owners... The adoption of procedural rules is necessary in relation to the election of a new President and a member of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission with a view to the expiry of the mandates of Mr. Ivan Ivanov and Mrs. Evgenia Haritonova. After the discussion and vote, the Energy Committee, with 12 votes, is asked by the National Assembly to examine and adopt the following draft decision: Project! DECISION adopting Rules of Procedure on the terms and conditions for the proposal of a President and a member of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission, presentation, public disclosure of documents and hearing within the Energy Committee and the procedure for election by the National Assembly The National Assembly on the basis of art. 86, para. 1 of the Constitution of the Republic of Bulgaria in relation to art. 11, para. 2 and 5 of the Energy Act and art. 89, para. 8 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly HAS DECIDED It shall adopt Rules of Procedure on the terms and conditions for the proposal of a President and a member of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission, presentation, public disclosure of documents and hearing in the Committee on Energy and the procedure for election by the National Assembly: I. Offering a Chair and one member of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission and submitting their documents. 1. Proposals for a Chair of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission and a member with an internship in the field of energy shall be made by MPs or by parliamentary groups. The proposals shall be submitted in writing to the Energy Commission through the President of the National Assembly within 5 days of the adoption of these rules. 2. The proposals for a Chair and a member with experience in the energy sector of the Commission for Energy and Water Regulatory shall contain written reasons to justify the good professional reputation of the candidate concerned. The proposal shall be accompanied by: (a) the written consent of the applicant to be proposed for the position under the model Annex 1 to the decision; (b) Declaration under art. 12, para. 1 of the Energy Act, that the applicant is a competent Bulgarian citizen according to the model "Annex No 2" to the decision; (c) written consent of the applicant under art. 27, para. 2 of the Act for Access and Disclosure of Documents and for the declaration of belonging of Bulgarian citizens to the State Security and the intelligence services of the Bulgarian People's Army according to the model Annex 3 to the Decision; (d) a detailed CV; (e) certified by the applicant a copy of a diploma for completed higher education with an educational-qualification degree of Master. (f) documents certifying that the applicant proposed for: (aa) Chair, shall meet the requirement for professional internship under art. 12, para. 1, t. 2 point (e) in relation to § 1, point (e) 59 or 59a of the Energy Act; (bb) a member with experience in the field of energy shall meet the requirement of professional experience under art. 12, para. 1, t. Paragraph 2 (a) is replaced by the following: 59 of the Energy Act; 3. The circumstances of art. 12, para. 1, t. 3 of the Energy Act is established by the administration of the National Assembly. II. Public disclosure of documents 1. The proposals, together with the documents attached to them, shall be published on the specialized thematic site on the website of the National Assembly no later than 2 days before the hearing. 2. The publication of the proposals and documents shall be subject to the Personal Data Protection Act and Regulation (EU) 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data and repealing Directive 95/46/EC (General Data Protection Regulation). (OJ L 119/1 of 4 May 2016) 3. Non-profit legal entities registered for public use activities and professional organisations, no later than three days before the hearing of candidates, may submit opinions to the Energy Commission on the proposed candidates, including issues to be addressed to them. 4. The mass media may send to the Commission on energy issues to the candidates to be assigned to them. 5. The questions sent by the persons on the item. 3 and 4 shall be notified to applicants in writing no later than 24 hours before the start of the hearing. 6. Anonymous opinions and alerts shall not be considered. 7. Opinions and questions shall be sent by post to the following address: gr. Sofia, p.k.a. 1169, p. Prince Alexander I No 1 addressed to the Chairman of the Energy Commission, or by e-mail: 47_KE@parliament.bg. Opinions and questions shall be published on the specialized thematic website of the National Assembly, subject to the Personal Data Protection Act, the Classified Information Protection Act and Regulation (EU) 2016/679 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 27 April 2016 on the protection of individuals with regard to the processing of personal data and on the free movement of such data and repealing Directive 95/46/EC (General Data Protection Regulation). (OJ L 119/1 of 4 May 2016) . III. Perform a preliminary check under the Law on Access and Disclosure of Documents and for the declaration of belonging of Bulgarian citizens to State Security and the intelligence services of the Bulgarian People's Army 1. Candidates shall be checked for belonging to the bodies referred to in art. 1 of the Law on Access and Disclosure of Documents and the declaration of belonging of Bulgarian citizens to State Security and the intelligence services of the Bulgarian People's Army. The request for prior verification under art. 27, para. 1, t. 2 of the Law on Access and Disclosure of Documents and the declaration of belonging of Bulgarian citizens to State Security and the intelligence services of the Bulgarian People's Army are made by the Chairman of the Energy Commission. 2. The results of the verification shall be published on the specialized thematic site on the website of the National Assembly. IV. Hearing of candidates for chairman or member with experience in the energy sector of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission. 1. Before hearing the proposed candidates, the Energy Commission shall verify the documents submitted and whether the applicant complies with the relevant requirements for taking up the position proposed by the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission. 2. The hearing of the admitted candidates is held in an open meeting of the Energy Committee, which is broadcast in real time on the Internet through the specialized thematic website of the National Assembly. 3. The candidates shall be submitted by the importers of the proposals in alphabetical order of the names of the applicants within two minutes of a candidate. The presentation also includes details of specific preparation, motivation, public reputation and public support for the applicant. 4. Personal presentation of candidates and their concept of the Commission's energy and water regulation activities as a regulatory authority, with a view to the position for which they are proposed to a candidate within 10 minutes. 5. The Energy Committee shall hold debates on the proposals made. The Members of the National Assembly shall place their questions separately to the candidates for the Chair and to the candidates for an Alphabetical member of their own names within two minutes of the Member of the National Assembly. Upon completion of the questions asked to each candidate by all Members of the National Assembly, he shall answer within 10 minutes. 6. The Chairman of the Energy Commission shall give the floor to the candidates to answer the questions asked to them by the persons referred to in art. 89, para. 4 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, to which no answer has been received during the hearing so far. Candidates answer within two minutes for each question. 7. Candidates shall have the right to comment on the opinions submitted within 5 minutes in total. 8. The Chair of the Energy Commission may request additional information from both the applicant and the relevant competent authority. 9. A full stenographic protocol is drawn up from the hearing, which is published on the specialized thematic website on the website of the National Assembly. 10. The Energy Committee shall draw up within the meaning of art. 89, para. 5 and para. 6 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, a report from the hearing to which a list of candidates involved in the hearing is attached, arranged in alphabetical order on their own names and submitted it to the National Assembly. The report shall be accompanied by: (a) draft decision to elect the Chair of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission for each of the candidates; (b) draft decision to elect a member with experience in the energy sector of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission for each of the candidates. 11. The refusal of a candidate to participate in the hearing or its withdrawal from the importer shall not suspend the procedure with regard to the other candidates for the position concerned. V. Election of a President and one member with experience in the energy sector of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission of the National Assembly. 1. The meeting is broadcast in real time on the Internet through the specialized thematic website of the National Assembly. The proposed candidates are present at the meeting by decision of the National Assembly. 2. The National Assembly shall hear the report of the Energy Committee. 3. Each candidate shall be represented by the applicant within two minutes. 4. Discussions on applications shall be held in accordance with the procedure laid down in Article 1. 52 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly. 5. The National Assembly shall elect separately the Chair of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission and one member with experience in the energy and water regulation Commission. 6. The vote is evident through the computerised voting system. If there are several candidates for one position, the vote shall be carried out in alphabetical order on the applicant's own names, unless the National Assembly decides otherwise. 7. The candidate who received more than half of the votes of the present Members of the National Assembly shall be considered elected. If several candidates, for the respective position, have received more than half of the votes of the present Members of the National Assembly, the candidate who has received the most votes for the House shall be elected. 8. Where none of the candidates for the position concerned receives the necessary votes, a re-vote shall take place, in which the two candidates who received the most votes for the position concerned shall take part. 9. If none of the candidates for the respective position receive the necessary votes in the second vote, the National Assembly shall adopt a decision to open a new selection procedure. 10. When one of the candidates refuses to participate in the election before voting on his candidacy by the National Assembly and he is the only candidate for office, the National Assembly shall adopt a decision to open a new procedure for the election of the Chair of the Commission for Energy and Water Regulatory or for one member with experience in the energy sector of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission. 11. If there is no candidate for any of the positions, the National Assembly shall adopt a decision to open a new selection procedure for this position. VI. Obligation to declare incompatibility under art. 35, para. 1, t. 1 and a change in the declared circumstances under art. 35, para. 1, t. 3 of the Law on Countering Corruption and the Withdrawal of Illegally Acquired Property. 1. Upon completion of the selection procedure under Section V, the elected President and a member of the Energy and Water Regulatory Commission shall submit a declaration of incompatibility under art. 35, para. 1, t. 1 of the Law on Countering Corruption and for the withdrawal of illegally acquired property in connection with art. 12, para. 2 and 3 of the Energy Act in template, according to Annex 4 to the Decision. 2. In the event of a change in the declared circumstances of the declaration referred to in point (a) of paragraph 1, the customs authorities of the Member States of the other Member States of the other Member States of the other Member States of the other Member States of the European Union of the other Member States of the European Union of the other Member States of the European Union of the European Union and of the other Member States of the European Union of the European Union of the European Union, of the one part, of the one part, of the other part, of the other part, of the European Union, of the other part, of the European Union, of the other part, of the other part, of the European Union, of the other part, of the European Union, of the European Union, of the European Union, of the European Union, of the one part, of the other part, of the other part, and of the other part, of the Republic of the other part, of the other part, of the

In this case, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure of art. 10, para. 1, t. 4 of Ordinance No 1 of 30 July 2003 on the nomenclature of types of constructions and art. 137, para. 1, t. The facility should be classified as a fifth-category construction which is required by law to issue a construction permit under the order of the LUT and not a permit for placing a moving object. In cases where movable facilities are placed without authorisation or contrary to one issued or do not comply with other requirements defined by the ordinance of the Municipal Council, the provisions of art. 57a, para. 3 of the LU provides that the mayor of the municipality concerned will issue an order for removal on the basis of a constitutional act drawn up by the municipal officials. In this regard, a letter has been sent repeatedly by the DNCC authorities requesting the mayor of Radomir Municipality to order action to detect and remove this mobile facility, as it does not constitute a moving object according to the provisions of the LOC. As you have mentioned, he has been fined for failing to comply with this order because he has not taken action as an official to detect and eliminate illegal construction. Unfortunately, the RDNC authorities are administrative bodies, they are not entitled to carry out procedural actions within the meaning of the Code of Criminal Procedure. Here I'd say the ball is the law enforcement field. This is about the second question you're asking me. In the field view, it was found that the sports facility was not removed, the front door was locked and the facility was not used as intended at the time of the inspection. During the verification in the municipality of Radomir by employees of the Municipal Construction Control Administration no data on the actions taken to detect and remove the facility in accordance with the orders given by the DNC are provided. In conclusion, it should be explicitly noted that the removal of the sports facility is entirely within the authority of the mayor of Radomir municipality in accordance with art. 57a of LU, such as the DNC, unfortunately, has no legal basis to take administrative action on the case. ParlaMint-BG_2022-01-21.u1344	Thank you, Mr. Simeonov. Here you go, Mr. Karadjov.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-01-21.u1345	Mr. Chairman, ladies and gentlemen of the House! Dear Mr. Simeonov, Motorway "Hemus" as I am sure that everyone in this hall you know is divided into ten subdivisions and the deadlines for completing each of these sections are different. It is therefore very difficult with one single deadline to say when it will be completed. If we are referring to the furthest period from today to the completion of the last section, yes, this is the late 2027 and in my next answer I will try to justify why. The time limits for the construction of the motorway are depending on the following legal procedures, namely: first, the preparation and approval of the PSU parcel plan for the route of the respective section; second, the conduct of an EIA procedure; third, the conduct of estrangement procedures, which usually almost 100% end with passing through the court, end with court cases; the preparation and approval of the technical project and the issuance of building permits. Only after the issue of construction permits is it possible to proceed to a competition under the Public Procurement Act and to move on to construction. However, in the contracts already concluded at the motorway of Hemus, the time limits for the construction of the various sections start to run from the opening of the construction site concerned and they are different for the different sections. For example: the deadline for completion of the CMR for a given section is 42 months from the opening of the construction site, and in others they are faster than 30 months. In any case, the opening of a construction site cannot be legally carried out without taking the above five steps. In the period 2018 - 2019, API has concluded contracts with 14, para. 1 t. 6 of the ZOP, for the construction of the 240 km remaining from the route of the highway. In the route of Boaz to the road node of Bukhovtsi. Currently, the objective deadlines for completion of the Motorway are as follows: Section I "Dermantsi" to the Dermantsi road hub, will be completed by August 2022; from road hub to the end of section one "Hemus" by April 2023; Section II will be completed by the end of 2024; Section III "Dermantsi road hub by June 2023; the construction of sections IV, V and VI from road hub Pleven to Veliko Tarnovo will start only after the final completion of the alienation procedures, the issuance of building permits, and here the estimated deadline for completion is 36 to 42 months from the opening of the construction site. For divisions VII, XVIII and IX from Veliko Tarnovo to road hub Bukhovtsi, the time limit for the construction part is 36 months from the opening of the construction site. [[Heckling]] In addition, I would point out that for divisions VII and VIII, a whole new environmental procedure is needed, a brand new parcel plan is being developed. New project solutions are being worked on and the route of division VII will be extended by about 3 km, and section VIII will be extended by about 4 km. There is also a procedure for a new environmental impact assessment due to the need to shift the initially approved route. The EIA procedure and approval of a new route shall last between 12 and 18 months after its award. The design of a larger number of facilities will be necessary to ensure crossings with the railway infrastructure. For precinct nine, we've got a technical project ready. Its construction is expected to start in 2023 and will be completed within 30 months of the opening of the construction site. The section from the road node Buchovtsi to the road node Belokopitovo is 16.3 km long. It is built under another contract after an open procedure under the Law of the Republic of Bulgaria and is at the final stage. There is no difficulty with it, there is a competition procedure of the ZOP. Abbreviation of the envisaged legal time limits for environmental, alienation, conciliation procedures, subject to the provisions of the laws in force, is not possible in their current editorials. ParlaMint-BG_2022-01-21.u1368	Thank you, Mr. Alexandrov. Here you are, Minister, for an answer.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-01-21.u1369	Mr. Chairman, ladies and gentlemen of the House! Dear Mr. Alexandrov, the main objective of the Law on Persons with Disabilities is to promote, protect and ensure the full and equal exercise of the rights of people with disabilities for their social inclusion and effective participation in public life, following the principles of equality and non-discrimination. In turn, the United Nations Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities as part of the Bulgarian legislation requires that an individual approach be applied when assessing disability and the possibilities for social inclusion. In this regard, a new model has been introduced for the formation of financial support for people with disabilities as a type of social payment, which aims to support certain groups of persons under specific regulatory requirements, taking into account the poverty line established for the country for the relevant year, which is a dynamic monetary indicator with an annual update. This puts people in a more favourable economic situation while creating a guarantee to avoid the possibility of reducing the amount of financial support for this social group. With regard to the differentiation laid down in the scope of the individual groups under art. 70 of the Law in the process of developing the Law on People with Disabilities has been made and proposals have been adopted regarding the introduction of new target groups within the scope of which to reflect as a regulatory condition a specific type of granted pension. In this regard, when determining the amount of monthly financial support, account shall be taken of the extent of the damage and of the hypotheses of art. 70, p. 4 and 5 of the Law as an additional condition is also laid down the type of pension received by the person at the time of granting the monthly financial support, which presupposes the intensity of support. When regulatoryly determining the amount of monthly financial support for people with permanent disabilities is taken into account and the need to provide support to the most vulnerable and dependent persons who, as a result of severe disabilities, have not had and will not have the opportunity to pursue employment, respectively, to receive income from labour and the relevant social security rights. These are the people in an extremely aggravated life situation, given the existing functional limitations and barriers. The division of the groups under art. 70 of the Law was introduced after active discussions and public consensus with interested parties on the topic. There is compliance with current health legislation. The law guarantees the rights of disabled people in a way that ensures their equal treatment according to personal needs without prioritising a status acquired under the order of another special law. Military invalids also enjoy rights provided for in special laws that further guarantee their preferences and services. The application of the existing norms guaranteeing the care of the state towards the military invalids is an indicative example of appreciation and therefore do not feel neglected. On this occasion, any findings of the deterioration of the military invalids on the occasion of their public support are unfounded. When ignoring the statutory principle introduced and extending the scope of the supported persons, the identified risks to such requests from other groups of persons should be taken into account. For example, from persons who receive a survivor's pension and/or a retirement and age pension, and so on, and the expected effectiveness of such a regulatory change. At the same time, the policy focus aimed at active social inclusion of disabled people should not be shifted, including those who have acquired the status of military disability in the predominance of social assistance. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-01-21.u1377	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Mr. Chairman, ladies and gentlemen of the House! The amendments adopted in the Criminal Code as of 1 January 2015 criminalise the evasion of compulsory social security contributions or health insurance. As provided for in para. 1 of art. 255b of the NC of the provision cited, which hides compulsory social security contributions for public insurance or for health insurance in large amounts by declaring a less insured income than the actual insured person, does not submit a declaration, confirms non-true or secret truth in a declaration lodged, compositions or uses a false document, non-true or falsified document in the exercise of a business activity, keeping accounting or submitting information to the revenue authorities or destroying, or hiding within statutory deadlines accounting documents, accounting records or payrolls, shall be punished with imprisonment up to 5 years and with a fine of up to 2 thousand. lv. Hiding social security contributions in large sizes from 3 to 12 thousand. BGN is punishable by imprisonment for up to 5 years with a fine of up to 2 thousand. lv. In the case of amounts in particularly large amounts, over 12 thousand. BGN, the penalty is imprisonment from 2 to 8 years and confiscation of part or all of the property of the guilty. This measure was introduced in order to prevent unlawful acts which cause significant damage to the proceeds of State social security funds and the health insurance system and constitute socially dangerous acts directly threatening the financial stability of the country. It ensures the fulfilment of the constitutional rights of citizens for social and health insurance, and the leading objective is to implement a general prevention in respect of compliance with the requirements of the insurance legislation. Notice, according to data provided by the National Revenue Agency of the Unified Electronic Register for the Notification of Institutions for the period from 1 January 2015 to 31 December 2021, the Revenue Authorities of the NRA have sent 24 notifications to the Prosecutor's Office of withholding compulsory social security contributions or for health insurance for destruction or evasion within statutory periods of accounting documents, accounting records or payroll bills, as well as for establishing indications of a criminal offence within the meaning of the NK, including for tax fraud committed. Data from the NRA registry show that 4 pre-trial proceedings were initiated as a result of the notifications sent, 1 pre-trial proceedings were terminated and there were 2 refusals to initiate pre-trial proceedings. Other than that, no information is available on other pre-trial proceedings and convictions under art. 255b of the NK and related follow-up.
